[资源] GPU 0/0 | Worker 2 (CPU 2: plan_035 Q3 inflation running, plan_036 Growing Pains just spawned) | 磁盘: local

## plan_034_multi_subject_injection 完成 ✅ (Round 5 最高优先级)

**Hypothesis**: Proposition 1 (ΔFPR=0) 在多 subject 同时污染下的 robustness boundary

**核心结果 — Non-Target ΔFPR (matching variable 污染导致无辜 subject 的 FPR 膨胀):**

| k (污染 subject 数) | p=0.1 | p=0.3 | p=0.5 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (1.8%) | +0.002 | +0.002 | +0.002 |
| 3 (5.3%) | +0.002 | +0.001 | −0.001 |
| 5 (8.8%) | +0.004 | −0.000 | +0.007 |
| **10 (17.5%)** | +0.001 | **+0.026** | **+0.069** |
| **20 (35.1%)** | +0.001 | **+0.072** | **+0.167** |

**Proposition 1 validity boundary: k ≈ 5–10 subjects**
- k ≤ 5 (≤8.8% subjects)：ΔFPR < 0.007，与噪声无异。Proposition 1 实际成立。
- k = 10 (17.5%)：p ≥ 0.3 时 ΔFPR 开始显著。
- k = 20 (35.1%)：p=0.5 时 ΔFPR = +0.167（绝对 FPR 从 0.31 升到 0.49）。

**Target TPR**: k=1→k=20 下降 0.834→0.611 (p=0.5)，matching variable 吸收 DIF 信号。

**p=0.1 全 k 值 robust**：即使 k=20，ΔFPR ≈ 0.001。

**对比 hypothesis**: 
- 预测 k≤3 ΔFPR<0.02 → ✅ 实际 k≤5 都 <0.007
- 预测 k≥10 ΔFPR>0.05 → ✅ k=10/p=0.5 ΔFPR=0.069
- 预测 ΔTPR 稳定 → ⚠️ TPR 有 dilution（0.834→0.611），但仍 >0.3

**Paper 用法**: 论文 Proposition 1 可声明 "ΔFPR=0 保证在 contamination 影响 <10% subjects 时实际成立（graceful degradation）"，同时透明报告 boundary 在 Limitations。

---

**进度**: plan_035 (Q3 inflation) 仍在运行，plan_036 (Growing Pains) 刚派出。DDL 5/23，当前按计划推进。

[Reviewer 回复]:
## 审查报告 — plan_034 Multi-Subject Injection

**结论**: PASS（1 个 info-level 注记）

### 代码审查

**实验设计** ✓：
- k ∈ {1,2,3,5,10,20} × p ∈ {0.1,0.3,0.5} × 5 seeds = 90 runs
- 包含 k=1 baseline（可与 plan_016 交叉验证）和 k=20 极端场景 ✓
- 每个 (k,p) 有 5 次随机 subject 选择，报告 mean±std ✓ —— 我之前建议的 subject selection protocol 被采纳

**注入机制** (line 303-307) ✓：
- 仅翻转 focal inject subset（accuracy overlap 内的 787/807 个 2024 模型）的 incorrect→correct
- `flip_mask = zeros & (rng.random(...) < p)` → 只翻转 0→1，不引入负向 DIF ✓

**匹配变量构造**（核心逻辑）✓：
- Target subject matching (line 338-340): non-target total + other-target injected — 正确排除被测 target 但包含 OTHER injected targets
- Non-target subject matching (line 373-374): (non-target total - this subject) + all injected target total — 正确包含所有 injected targets 来测试 leakage
- 这是测试 Proposition 1 边界的正确设置

**效率优化** ✓：
- 预计算 per-subject totals 避免重复矩阵运算
- k>5 时 non-target 采样 20/N 个 subject（line 362-367），合理的计算量控制
- Strata merging 保证每层 ≥5 样本（line 166-185）

### 数值核实

summary_by_k.csv 与 report 表格数值一致 ✓。

**ΔFPR 退化曲线清晰且可解释**：
- k≤5, 所有 p: |ΔFPR| < 0.007 → Proposition 1 实际成立
- k=10, p=0.5: ΔFPR = +0.069 → 边界区域
- k=20, p=0.5: ΔFPR = +0.167 → 明确失效
- p=0.1 全程 robust（k=20 仍仅 +0.001）

**TPR dilution 符合预期**：p=0.5 时 TPR 从 0.834 (k=1) 降至 0.611 (k=20)

### 发现

**[severity: info] inf-delta 处理不一致但不影响 ΔFPR 结论**

plan_034 使用 `else np.inf`（inf-delta → C），而 plan_001/028 使用 `else 0.0`（inf-delta → A）。全 codebase 扫描发现两派分裂：

- `else 0.0`（→A）: plan_001, 010, 022, 024, 027, 028
- `else np.inf`（→C）: plan_011, 012, 014, 016, 034
- `else 999.0`（→C）: plan_004

plan_034 的 ΔFPR 是 **internally consistent**（baseline 和 injection 都用 `np.inf`），所以退化曲线的结论不受影响。但 plan_034 的 absolute FPR（≈0.319）与 plan_001 的 C%（31.32%）有 ~0.6pp 偏差，部分由此导致。

对论文影响：无——论文引用的 31.3% 来自 plan_001（`else 0.0`），plan_034 只用 ΔFPR（相对量）。但建议 code release 时统一处理方式。

**[severity: info] Registry 未注册** — 持续性模式，不再单独报 warning。

### 论文写法建议

plan_034 的结果支持以下论文表述：

> Cross-subject DIF auditing maintains ΔFPR ≈ 0 when contamination affects ≤ 9% of test subjects (k ≤ 5 of 57). Beyond this boundary, ΔFPR increases with the product of contaminated fraction and injection intensity, reaching +0.069 at k=10 (17.5%) with p=0.5. Proposition 1's guarantee degrades gracefully under realistic contamination scenarios.

建议在 Proposition 1 旁加 scope 限定词："under the assumption that the matching variable subjects are uncontaminated (empirically validated up to ~9% simultaneous contamination; see §4.3)"。

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	We adopt Differential Item Functioning (DIF) analysis from educational measurement to assess whether individual benchmark items behave consistently across groups of LLMs. The procedure requires only a binary response matrix and group labels, with no access to model internals or training data. Below we define the data, the statistical test, the cohort designs, and a semi-synthetic validation framework.
5	
6	\subsection{Data}
7	\label{sec:data}
8	
9	The primary dataset is the METABENCH response matrix~\citep{kipnis2024metabench}, which records binary outcomes (1~=~correct, 0~=~incorrect) for 5,227 models evaluated on 12,508 MMLU items~\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu}. Models span release dates from 2023 to 2024, drawn from the Open LLM Leaderboard. MMLU covers 57 subjects grouped into five broad domains (STEM, Humanities, Social Sciences, Other, and a mixed category). We supplement the response matrix with the web-overlap contamination labels of \citet{li2024contamination}, which classify 9,229 of 12,508 items (73.8\% coverage) as ``contaminated'' or ``clean'' based on verbatim matches in publicly accessible web corpora. These labels serve as an external reference for evaluating the relationship between DIF flags and existing contamination indicators.
10	
11	\subsection{Mantel-Haenszel DIF and ETS Classification}
12	\label{sec:mhdif}
13	
14	In educational testing, Differential Item Functioning (DIF) occurs when an item's statistical relationship to the latent trait $\theta$ differs between two groups after conditioning on ability level. An item with DIF is not merely harder for one group; it functions differently relative to what the group's overall ability would predict. The standard terminology refers to test-takers as ``examinees'' and questions as ``items.'' In our setting, examinees are LLMs and items are benchmark questions.
15	
16	The Mantel-Haenszel (MH) procedure~\citep{holland1988mh} detects DIF by stratifying examinees into $K$ score bins based on total score (a proxy for $\theta$) and computing a common odds ratio across strata. We use $K = 5$ equiprobable strata (quintile-based binning) following ETS recommended practice for large-scale assessments~\citep{donoghue1993mh,clauser1998dif}. Within each stratum $k$, the responses of the reference group and the focal group form a $2 \times 2$ contingency table:
17	
18	\begin{equation}
19	\label{eq:mh}
20	\alpha_{\mathrm{MH}} = \frac{\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K} A_k D_k \,/\, T_k}{\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K} B_k C_k \,/\, T_k}\,,
21	\end{equation}
22	
23	\noindent where $A_k$ and $B_k$ are the number of correct and incorrect responses in the reference group for stratum $k$, $C_k$ and $D_k$ are the corresponding counts for the focal group, and $T_k$ is the total number of examinees in stratum $k$. Under the null hypothesis of no DIF, $\alpha_{\mathrm{MH}} = 1$. The log odds ratio is converted to the ETS delta scale:
24	
25	\begin{equation}
26	\label{eq:delta}
27	\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}} = -2.35 \ln(\alpha_{\mathrm{MH}})\,.
28	\end{equation}
29	
30	The Educational Testing Service (ETS) classifies DIF severity into three categories based on $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}|$. Category~A (negligible) requires $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| < 1.0$. Category~C (large) requires both $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ and statistical significance at $p < 0.05$ from the MH chi-squared test. Category~B (moderate) includes all remaining items: those with $1.0 \leq |\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| < 1.5$, or $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ without statistical significance. The dual threshold in ETS~C (effect size \emph{and} significance) guards against flagging items that show large but unreliable effect sizes. Throughout this paper, we report the percentage of items classified as ETS~C, denoted C\%. For example, if 2024 models consistently outperform 2023 models on a formal-logic item \emph{after controlling for overall ability}, the pooled odds ratio $\alpha_{\mathrm{MH}} = 0.42$ yields $\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}} = -2.35 \ln(0.42) = 2.04$, classified as ETS~C ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$, $p < 0.001$).
31	
32	The choice of matching variable (the total score used for stratification) affects both validity and interpretation. We distinguish three matching strategies: (1)~\emph{standard matching} uses the within-benchmark total score on all items of the same benchmark; (2)~\emph{cross-subject matching} uses the total score on all subjects \emph{other than} the one containing the item under test, preventing the matching variable from absorbing injected contamination signals (\Cref{sec:injection}); and (3)~\emph{external matching} uses the total score on a different benchmark entirely (e.g., MMLU total score as the matching variable when testing DIF on GSM8K items), providing an independent ability proxy uncontaminated by the target benchmark. Cross-subject and external matching both address matching variable contamination but at different granularities: cross-subject operates within a multi-domain benchmark, while external operates across benchmarks.
33	
34	\paragraph{LLM-specific challenges.} Applying MH-DIF to LLMs introduces assumptions absent in educational testing. First, models trained on overlapping corpora violate local independence; Yen's Q3 analysis confirms this is bounded ($r = 0.09$, $p = 0.50$; \Cref{app:method_details}). Second, fine-tuned variants and merges within model families are not independent examinees; graduated de-duplication shows family structure does not inflate C\% (\Cref{sec:structural}). Third, the ability distribution is non-normal (bimodal from base vs.\ instruction-tuned models), motivating quintile-based rather than parametric stratification. Fourth, MMLU's 57 subjects span multiple latent dimensions, while MH assumes unidimensionality; cross-subject matching and within-subject DIF checks address this (\Cref{sec:structural}).
35	
36	\subsection{Cohort Designs}
37	\label{sec:cohorts}
38	
39	We construct five cohort comparisons: (1)~\emph{Temporal}: 2023 ($N = 1{,}080$) vs.\ 2024 ($N = 807$) models, the primary comparison; (2)~\emph{Ability-quintile}: Q1--Q2 vs.\ Q4--Q5 within the same temporal window, testing whether ability differences alone produce DIF; (3)~\emph{Within-family}: Llama-2 ($N = 161$) vs.\ Llama-3 ($N = 308$), isolating generational changes within a single family; (4)~\emph{Within-subject}: per-subject DIF to test domain concentration; and (5)~\emph{Random split}: two random halves of the 2023 cohort ($N = 540$ each) as a sanity check.
40	
41	\subsection{Semi-Synthetic Injection Framework}
42	\label{sec:injection}
43	
44	To evaluate whether MH-DIF can detect differential contamination when it is present, we construct a semi-synthetic framework with controlled ground truth.
45	
46	\paragraph{Procedure.} From items classified as ETS~A in the uninjected data (negligible DIF), we select 20\% uniformly at random as the ``contaminated'' set. For each contaminated item, we flip incorrect responses to correct in the focal group at exploitation rate $p \in \{0.0, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\}$. We then run MH-DIF on the modified response matrix and measure detection performance. We report $\Delta\mathrm{TPR} = \mathrm{TPR}(\text{injected}) - \mathrm{TPR}(\text{baseline})$ and $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = \mathrm{FPR}(\text{injected}) - \mathrm{FPR}(\text{baseline})$, where TPR is the true positive rate on injected items and FPR is the false positive rate on uninjected items. Each injection level is repeated across 5 random seeds for uncertainty estimation.
47	
48	\paragraph{Cross-subject matching.} The choice of matching variable is critical for valid semi-synthetic evaluation. When contamination is injected into items within subject $X$, the total score on subject $X$ absorbs part of the injection signal. Using this contaminated total score as the MH matching variable violates the conditional independence assumption and inflates FPR. Cross-subject matching resolves this by computing the matching variable from all subjects \emph{other than} the one being tested.
49	
50	\begin{proposition}[Zero FPR under single-subject cross-subject matching]
```

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We apply MH-DIF to the METABENCH response matrix~\citep{kipnis2024metabench} (5,227 models $\times$ 12,508 MMLU items~\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu}), with temporal cohorts defined by release date (2023 vs.\ 2024). Cross-benchmark validation across all six METABENCH benchmarks appears in \Cref{sec:gsm8k}.
5	
6	\subsection{Validation: DIF Detection Power}
7	\label{sec:validation}
8	
9	A Monte Carlo power analysis (1,000 replications per condition) using empirical 2PL parameters from PSN-IRT~\citep{zhou2026psnirt} ($\bar{a} = 1.2$, $\bar{b} = -0.84$; 14,042 MMLU items) confirms that at $N \geq 80$ per cohort, ETS~C achieves TPR of 0.68--0.80, FPR of 0.036--0.042, and AUC of 0.93--0.94 for items with $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.4$ (details in \Cref{app:method_details}). Our temporal cohorts ($N_{\mathrm{ref}} = 1{,}080$, $N_{\mathrm{focal}} = 807$) far exceed this threshold.
10	
11	\subsection{Temporal Comparability: 31\% Measurement Drift}
12	\label{sec:temporal}
13	
14	We compare 2023 ($N = 1{,}080$) against 2024 ($N = 807$) models; 31.3\% of items receive ETS~C classification ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$; 95\% bootstrap CI: [29.4\%, 38.1\%], $B = 1{,}000$ model-level resamples). Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction at $q = 0.05$ removes only 3 items (31.32\% $\to$ 31.30\%), because the dual ETS threshold on effect size and significance already provides implicit multiple-testing protection.
15	
16	Three controls confirm this reflects genuine temporal change. A random split of the 2023 cohort yields only 0.18\% ETS~C items (sampling noise), and an ability-quintile comparison within the same temporal cohort yields 2.9\% (ability differences alone). A within-family comparison (Llama-2 vs.\ Llama-3) produces 61.3\% ETS~C items, elevated by large ability gaps that confound the DIF signal~\citep{holland1988mh}. The contrast between 2.9\% (ability-controlled) and 61.3\% (ability-confounded) brackets the temporal rate of 31.3\%. Crucially, the temporal cohort ability gap is negligible: Cohen's $d = -0.17$ (below the $|d| = 0.2$ small-effect threshold) with 76.4\% distributional overlap. Under strict 1:1 nearest-neighbor matching ($d \approx -0.010$, $N = 638$ per group, 5 seeds), C\% $= 28.9\% \pm 0.1\%$---a 7.7\% relative reduction from 31.3\%, confirming that at most $\sim$2.4 pp of observed DIF is attributable to ability mismatch.
17	
18	Across all 57 subjects, C\% ranges from 20.7\% to 48.4\% (mean $\approx 30\%$; \Cref{tab:temporal_landscape}). DIF rates are domain-invariant (Kruskal-Wallis $p = 0.78$), but DIF \emph{direction} exhibits domain dependence: STEM items favor 2024 models (55.4\%), Humanities favor 2023 (64.4\%). Item-level regression shows 92.4\% of the DIF signal is item-specific (pseudo $R^2 = 0.0007$). A Simpson's paradox confirms item properties---not accuracy gains---drive DIF: domain accuracy positively predicts DIF at the aggregate level ($\mathrm{OR} = 1.55$, $p = 0.001$) but reverses after controlling for item difficulty and discrimination ($\mathrm{OR} = 0.73$, $p = 0.030$). The conclusion is robust across thresholds: C\% $= 48.9\%$ at $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.0$, $18.3\%$ at $\geq 2.0$, $10.2\%$ at $\geq 2.5$; ETS~C's dual threshold makes results less sensitive to the number of strata $K$ than significance-only approaches ($K = 5$ per ETS practice~\citep{donoghue1993mh}).
19	
20	\begin{table}[t]
21	\centering
22	\caption{DIF landscape across cohort definitions. ETS~C denotes the percentage of items with $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$. FPR is measured against Li et al.\ web-overlap labels on the ``clean'' (non-overlapping) subset. Enrichment is the ratio of observed to expected overlap between DIF flags and web-overlap labels.}
23	\label{tab:temporal_landscape}
24	\small
25	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
26	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
27	\toprule
28	Cohort Definition & $N_{\mathrm{ref}}$ / $N_{\mathrm{focal}}$ & C\% & FPR (clean) & Enrich. \\
29	\midrule
30	Temporal (2023 vs.\ 2024) & 1,080 / 807 & 31.3 & 0.318 & 0.964 \\
```

```text
48	\paragraph{Cross-subject matching.} The choice of matching variable is critical for valid semi-synthetic evaluation. When contamination is injected into items within subject $X$, the total score on subject $X$ absorbs part of the injection signal. Using this contaminated total score as the MH matching variable violates the conditional independence assumption and inflates FPR. Cross-subject matching resolves this by computing the matching variable from all subjects \emph{other than} the one being tested.
49	
50	\begin{proposition}[Zero FPR under single-subject cross-subject matching]
51	\label{prop:fpr}
52	Let $M_i^{(-s)} = \sum_{j \notin s} Y_{ij}$ be the matching variable for examinee $i$ on focal subject $s$. Under single-subject injection (contamination confined to subject $s$), the tested item belongs to $s$ and does not contribute to $M_i^{(-s)}$; injecting contamination into $s$ leaves $M_i^{(-s)}$ unchanged. Therefore $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0$ by construction.
53	\end{proposition}
54	
55	When contamination spans multiple subjects simultaneously, $M_i^{(-s)}$ may absorb signal from other contaminated subjects, potentially inflating FPR. Empirically, this guarantee extends to multi-domain contamination affecting $\leq$9\% of subjects ($k \leq 5$), with graceful degradation beyond this boundary (\Cref{sec:semisynthetic}).
56	
57	Empirically, $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$ across all five MMLU domains (bootstrap 95\% CI $= [0.000, 0.000]$, 5,000 resamples), while detection power is preserved ($\Delta\mathrm{TPR} > 0.3$ at $p = 0.5$ for four of five subjects). We adopt cross-subject matching as the standard protocol for all semi-synthetic analyses.
58	
59	Diagnostic procedures (Yen's Q3 local independence, domain-specificity analysis, DIF decomposition) and statistical details (multiple testing correction, threshold sensitivity, architecture heterogeneity checks) appear in \Cref{app:method_details}.
60	
```

```text
48	\label{fig:dose_response}
49	\end{figure}
50	
51	\subsection{Structural Analysis}
52	\label{sec:structural}
53	
54	Three structural controls fail to reduce temporal C\% below 31\%: matched-ability quintiles, IRT $\theta$-conditioning ($r(\theta, \mathrm{total\ score}) = 0.9986$), and within-subject DIF (C\% $= 38.1\%$). Logistic regression DIF confirms 92.1\% concordance with MH and reveals additional non-uniform DIF (24.8\%), making 31\% conservative. Graduated de-duplication ($K{=}10$: 22.7\%; $K{=}20$: 29.1\%) confirms family structure does not inflate C\%. Ability-matched analysis reduces Llama-2 vs.\ Llama-3 C\% from 61.3\% to $28.3\%$ and temporal C\% from 31.3\% to $28.9\%$---consistent matched values ($\sim$28--29\%) despite vastly different unmatched rates, suggesting genuine item-level DIF accounts for $\sim$28--29\% of items. Six alternative explanations (ability confounding, family inflation, $\theta$-conditioning, within-subject effects, ranking impact, local dependence) were tested; none reduces C\% below $\sim$28\% (\Cref{app:structural}).
55	
56	\subsection{Semi-Synthetic Framework Validation}
57	\label{sec:semisynthetic}
58	
59	The critical methodological finding is that the choice of matching strategy determines validity. Cross-subject matching computes the conditioning variable from subjects \emph{other than} the one being tested, achieving $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$ across all five MMLU subjects while four of five produce $\Delta\mathrm{TPR} > 0.3$ at $p = 0.5$. Within-subject matching inflates FPR by ${+}0.186$ on average because the matching variable absorbs part of the injection signal. We extend to simultaneous multi-subject injection ($k \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 20\}$ subjects, 5 random draws each): cross-subject matching maintains $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} < 0.007$ for $k \leq 5$ ($\leq$9\% of MMLU domains); at $k = 10$ (18\%), $\Delta\mathrm{FPR}$ rises to 0.026--0.069, indicating the boundary of matching variable integrity (\Cref{app:method_details}). We adopt cross-subject matching as the standard protocol (\Cref{app:fig_matching}).
60	
61	\subsection{Downstream Impact}
62	\label{sec:downstream}
63	
64	Spearman correlation between full and stable-item rankings is $\rho \approx 0.997$: aggregate ordering is preserved despite 31\% measurement drift. DIF-flagged items nonetheless concentrate discriminative signal ($z = -8\sigma$ to $-49\sigma$ vs.\ random removal), though a circularity ablation confirms DIF-based removal is the \emph{least} disruptive non-random strategy. Model families show distinctive DIF profiles: Llama-3 derivatives gain ${+}53$ median rank positions, Mistral loses $35$ (\Cref{app:fig_downstream}). Removing C-flagged items disproportionately disadvantages 2024 models (cohort gap $= 96.2$ positions, $40\times$ larger than random removal; $p < 0.001$), confirming that DIF items carry concentrated inter-generational signal.
65	
66	\subsection{Cross-Benchmark Validation}
67	\label{sec:gsm8k}
```

_source line 786 · 2026-05-21T16:14:59.276Z_

[Reviewer 回复]:
## 审查报告 — plan_035 Q3 Local Dependence Inflation Simulation

**结论**: PASS

### 方法学核实

**实验设计优秀**：
- Permutation null（保留 LD）+ Independence control（破坏 LD）的对比设计干净地隔离了 LD 对 FPR 的贡献 ✓
- 1000 permutations × 8 subjects（Q3 范围 4.6%–34.2%）→ 充分覆盖 LD 频谱 ✓
- Cross-subject matching（line 377-381）与 plan_016 一致 ✓
- Independence control 通过列独立 shuffle 破坏 LD 同时保留 marginal difficulty ✓

**代码审查**：
- 分层固定不变（cross_scores 不受 within-subject permutation 影响）✓
- Vectorized MH 实现（line 84-131）正确：A=w@X_k 高效计算 ref-correct 向量 ✓
- Seed 管理（BASE_SEED + subj_idx×100000 + pi）→ 无交叉污染 ✓
- inf-delta 处理用 `np.inf`（line 69），与 plan_034 一致 ✓

### 数值核实

inflation_summary.csv 与 report 表格一致 ✓。

**核心发现**：
- **ETS-C FPR 极低**：所有 subject 的 FPR < 0.003，LD inflation 最大仅 +0.0010pp（marketing）。ETS 双准则（效应量+显著性）天然抑制了 LD 膨胀。
- **p<0.05 FPR 校准良好**：mean 0.0412（vs nominal 0.05），LD inflation 反而为负（-0.0019pp）——LD 使检验略微保守，非膨胀
- **Q3 与 inflation 无相关**：ρ=-0.238 (p=0.570)——即使 moral_scenarios（34.2% Q3>0.20）也未出现 FPR 升高

### 发现

[severity: info] **结论可信，直接回应 SF-6**。论文可以如此表述：

> "Permutation simulation (1,000 iterations × 8 subjects spanning Q3 = 4.6–34.2%) confirms that cross-subject matching combined with the ETS-C dual criterion (|Δ_MH| ≥ 1.5 and p < 0.05) produces negligible LD-attributable FPR inflation (<0.001 pp on average), even for subjects with >30% item pairs exceeding |Q3| > 0.20."

[severity: info] **小注**：8 个 subject 是按 Q3 水平有目的地选取的（非随机样本），report 中 "mean across 8 subjects" 不应解读为总体均值。但由于覆盖了 Q3 的极端高值（34.2%）且该条件下仍无膨胀，结论的一般性不受影响。

[severity: info] Registry 模式照旧。

```text
12	
13	## Review Mode
14	Full (2 Reviewers, 4-step roundtable)
15	
16	---
17	
18	## 1. Paper Summary
19	
20	The paper applies Mantel-Haenszel Differential Item Functioning (MH-DIF) to the METABENCH response matrix (5,227 models × 12,508 MMLU items), finding 31.3% of items exhibit large temporal DIF (ETS Category C) between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts—a rate 142× above the permutation null (z=622, p<0.001). The instability replicates across six benchmarks (17.9–31.3%), is not explained by capability improvement (≤2.4 pp; R²=0.0007), and is not driven by model non-independence or architecture heterogeneity. The paper critiques web-overlap labels as fundamentally mismatched for item-level differential detection (enrichment≈1.0), proposes a semi-synthetic validation framework with cross-subject matching (ΔFPR=0.000), and positions DIF as a diagnostic lens rather than a quality filter.
21	
22	Four claimed contributions: (1) temporal instability characterization, (2) ground truth meta-critique, (3) semi-synthetic validation framework, (4) DIF as diagnostic lens.
23	
24	---
25	
26	## 2. Reviewer Assignments
27	
28	- **R1** — Methods & Experiments (Soundness focus)
29	- **R2** — Novelty & Related Work (Contribution focus)
30
```

```text
7	
8	# Dream Review Report: Temporal Item Instability in LLM Benchmarks
9	
10	**Paper:** "Temporal Item Instability in LLM Benchmarks: A Large-Scale Differential Item Functioning Analysis"
11	**Venue:** EMNLP 2026 (ARR)
12	**Review Mode:** Full (2 Reviewers + Roundtable)
13	**Date:** 2026-05-21
14	
15	---
16	
17	## Roundtable Summary
18	
19	Two reviewers independently assessed the paper, then exchanged reviews and responded. Both converged on **Overall Assessment 3.5 (Borderline Conference)**. The roundtable identified three critical issues requiring revision and reached consensus on the paper's position: a well-executed empirical study with useful but incremental insights, currently better suited for Findings than Main.
20	
21	### Convergence Points
22	- Both reviewers independently scored Overall 3.5
23	- Three mandatory revision issues identified unanimously: (1) multidimensionality robustness, (2) direct ConStat/DVD comparison, (3) temporal window sensitivity
24	- Simpson's paradox (OR reversal 1.55→0.73) recognized as the most novel analytical contribution
25	- Cross-benchmark replication across 6 benchmarks is a genuine strength
26	- Honest reporting of negative results (bifactor IRT failure, unexecuted experiments) is appreciated
27	
28	### Key Disagreements (Resolved)
29	- **Proposition 1 (ΔFPR=0):** R1 initially viewed cross-subject matching as "genuine methodological contribution"; R2 called it "trivially true." After exchange, R1 conceded Proposition 1 is overstated as a formal proposition, though the implementation has reuse value.
30	- **Excitement:** R1 gave 3.5, R2 gave 3. R2 argued "more rigorous measurement of a known phenomenon" caps excitement at 3. Not fully resolved — averaged to 3.25.
```

```text
30	- **Excitement:** R1 gave 3.5, R2 gave 3. R2 argued "more rigorous measurement of a known phenomenon" caps excitement at 3. Not fully resolved — averaged to 3.25.
31	- **"Instability ≠ Invalidity":** R2 framed this as self-limiting ("so what?"); R1 defended it as actionable (maintainers can clean items without sacrificing discriminative power). Both agree the audit protocol needs concrete validation.
32	
33	---
34	
35	## R1 Review: Methods and Experiments
36	
37	### Summary
38	The paper applies MH-DIF to the METABENCH response matrix (5,227 models × 12,508 MMLU items) and finds 31.3% temporal instability, replicated across 6 benchmarks. Controls are thorough and causal claims appropriately hedged, but the multidimensionality concern and missing direct comparisons with existing detectors prevent a clear accept recommendation.
39	
40	### Strengths
41	1. **Scale and thoroughness of controls.** Six distinct controls (random split, ability-quintile, within-family, matched-ability, architecture, within-subject) systematically rule out confounds. The convergence of matched C% around 28–29% across different analyses is compelling.
42	2. **Cross-subject matching is a genuine methodological contribution.** ΔFPR=0.000 with bootstrap confirmation has clear reuse value for semi-synthetic DIF evaluation in multi-domain benchmarks.
43	3. **Appropriate hedging of causal claims.** GSM1K correlation labeled exploratory with wide CIs. Simpson's paradox presented as decomposition, not causal mechanism.
44	4. **Cross-benchmark replication** across 6 benchmarks (C% 17.9–31.3%, all random controls <1%) substantially raises confidence this is not MMLU-specific.
45	5. **Semi-synthetic dose-response validation.** Monotonic ΔTPR relationship with exploitation rate provides clean internal validity.
46	6. **Honest reporting of failures.** Bifactor IRT convergence failure, underpowered GSM1K, and explicit listing of unexecuted experiments demonstrate scientific transparency.
47	
48	### Weaknesses
49	1. **MH validity under severe multidimensionality (CRITICAL).** CFI=0.681 for MMLU IRT fit. Within-subject DIF C%=38.1% exceeds overall temporal C%=31.3% — removing cross-subject dependence structure *increases* detected DIF, suggesting total-score conditioning may mask multidimensionality artifacts rather than control for them. The ability-quintile control (C%=2.9%) does not address dimension-specific cohort differences.
50	2. **Semi-synthetic injection has limited ecological validity.** Uniform exploitation rate p across all items/models is a crude model. Real contamination is heterogeneous — varies by item difficulty, model capability, and contamination mechanism. No heterogeneous injection schedules explored.
51	3. **Per-family stratified permutation not executed.** Model families cluster by release date (2023=Llama-2 heavy, 2024=Llama-3 heavy). Simple label permutation may not preserve this structure. Graduated de-duplication (K=10: 22.7%, K=20: 29.1%) partially addresses but is not equivalent.
52	4. **No direct ConStat/DVD empirical comparison.** Claims 2–3 implicitly position DIF against existing detectors, but the comparison is entirely conceptual. Running ConStat on the same METABENCH data is feasible and would be far more convincing.
53	5. **Downstream impact circularity.** DIF removal compared only against random removal and accuracy-gap removal — no comparison against psychometrically principled alternatives (IRT-based discrimination filtering, scalability coefficient filtering).
54	6. **Wide bootstrap CI [29.4%, 38.1%].** 9-percentage-point spread suggests substantial model-level heterogeneity in DIF patterns. Implications not discussed.
55	7. **Coarse temporal cohort definition.** Calendar year conflates architecture, training data, alignment, and scale differences.
56	
57	### Questions for Authors
58	1. Why does within-subject C% (38.1%) exceed overall temporal C% (31.3%)? Does total-score conditioning suppress or mask DIF signal?
59	2. For Simpson's paradox: is item difficulty alone sufficient for the OR reversal, or is discrimination also needed? Could difficulty be a mediator rather than confounder?
60	3. Why is 6-month window C% (34.35%) higher than full-year (31.3%)? Is this a sample size effect or non-monotonic temporal relationship?
61	4. What is the item-level correlation between |Δ_MH| and item difficulty? Does DIF concentrate at difficulty extremes (floor/ceiling interaction)?
62	5. Were the same model cohorts used across all 6 benchmarks?
63	6. How sensitive is ΔFPR=0 to cross-subject transfer effects (training on subject X improving subject Y)?
64	
65	### Missing References
66	- Wainer & Wang (2000) on testlet DIF — relevant to MMLU's subject-clustered structure
67	- Penfield & Camilli (2007) DIF handbook chapter
68	- Polo et al. (2025) on LLM benchmark reliability
69	
70	### Scores
71	- **Soundness:** 3.5/5
72	- **Excitement:** 3.5/5
73	- **Reproducibility:** 4/5
74	- **Overall Assessment:** 3.5 (Borderline Conference)
75	- **Confidence:** 4/5
76	
77	---
78	
79	## R2 Review: Novelty and Related Work
80	
81	### Summary
82	The paper applies standard MH-DIF without modification to LLM benchmarks at unprecedented scale (5,227 models, 27,125 items across 6 benchmarks). Empirically thorough with useful conceptual contributions (ground truth meta-critique, Simpson's paradox), but core methodological novelty is limited and the "instability ≠ invalidity" framing is self-limiting.
83	
84	### Strengths
85	1. **Scale of empirical analysis is unprecedented** for psychometric analysis of LLM benchmarks. Six alternative explanations systematically tested and ruled out.
86	2. **Ground truth meta-critique is a genuine contribution.** The distinction between global exposure (web-overlap) and differential functioning (DIF), supported by enrichment≈1.0, Jaccard=0.206, phi=0.012, could influence how future contamination detectors are evaluated.
87	3. **Simpson's paradox finding is the most novel contribution.** OR reversal from 1.55 to 0.73 after item-property conditioning is genuinely surprising and adds real insight.
88	4. **Difficulty-direction interaction (ρ=−0.597)** — capability gains on hard items, erosion on easy items — suggests DIF captures genuine temporal construct shift.
89	5. **Related work is comprehensive and well-structured.** Four clearly delineated areas with appropriate cross-disciplinary coverage.
90	6. **Cross-benchmark two-tier pattern** (knowledge 27–31% vs skill 18–19%) is interpretable and adds structural insight.
91	
92	### Weaknesses
93	1. **Core method is entirely borrowed.** MH-DIF (1959/1988) applied without modification. Paper states this explicitly. For EMNLP 2026, "old method, new domain" typically requires either non-trivial adaptation or transformative empirical findings. The findings are interesting but the paper itself argues they don't threaten validity.
94	2. **Proposition 1 (ΔFPR=0) is trivially true.** Disjoint sets observation dressed as formal proposition inflates perceived methodological contribution. Cross-subject matching has psychometric precedent (Nandakumar 1993, Donoghue & Allen 1993).
95	3. **"Instability ≠ invalidity" is self-limiting.** If 31% of items can be removed without affecting rankings, the practical significance is unclear. The audit protocol (§5) is generic and unvalidated.
96	4. **No direct empirical comparison with ConStat/DVD.** Conceptual argument is logically sound but not as convincing as direct evidence on the same data.
97	5. **Temporal cohort conflates many variables.** DIF says "that" items function differently, not "why." Agnosticism about mechanism limits diagnostic utility for benchmark maintenance.
98	6. **GSM1K correlation underpowered (N=33) and over-interpreted.** Wide CI [−0.71, −0.16] given substantial discussion space relative to evidential weight.
99	7. **Missing head-to-head with Growing Pains.** Complementarity claim is conceptual, not demonstrated.
100	
101	### Questions for Authors
102	1. Can you run ConStat on the METABENCH matrix and report item-level agreement with DIF flags?
103	2. What is C% when using training data cutoff dates rather than model release dates?
104	3. Is the difficulty-direction interaction (ρ=−0.597) driven by ceiling/floor effects?
105	4. Can you demonstrate the audit protocol would have identified a known problem item retrospectively?
106	5. How sensitive is 31% to K=5 strata choice? Results at K=10, K=20?
107	6. Within-family Llama-2 vs Llama-3 (matched C%=28.3%): is residual DIF or residual confounding?
108	
109	### Missing References
```

```text
30	
31	---
32	
33	## 3. Independent Reviews
34	
35	### R1: Methods & Experiments
36	
37	#### Strengths
38	1. **Exceptionally thorough confound elimination (§4.3).** Six potential confounds systematically tested: architecture (99.98% decoder-only, Jaccard=1.0), ability gap (d=−0.17, matched C%=28.9%, 2.4pp confound), local independence (Q3=0.009), model non-independence (family-cap C%≤random at every K), IRT θ-conditioning (r=0.9986), non-uniform DIF (24.8% additional). The convergence of temporal matching (28.9%) and within-family Llama-2v3 matching (28.3%) at ~29% genuine DIF is particularly convincing.
39	2. **Cross-subject matching achieves clean ΔFPR=0.000 (§3.3, Proposition 1).** The insight that within-subject matching absorbs injected contamination signal is subtle and practically important. Empirical confirmation across 5 subjects is well-executed.
40	3. **Ground truth meta-critique is well-argued (§4.5).** Enrichment=0.964, Jaccard=0.206, φ=0.012 convincingly demonstrate orthogonality between DIF flags and web-overlap labels. The dose-response curve confirms DIF can detect differential contamination when present.
41	4. **Scale is unprecedented.** 5,227 × 12,508 on MMLU, replicated across 6 benchmarks totaling ~27,000 items.
42	5. **Simpson's paradox (Table 4) is compelling.** OR reversal from 1.55→0.73 (LR χ²=647) cleanly demonstrates item properties, not accuracy gains, drive DIF classification.
43	6. **Difficulty×direction interaction (ρ=−0.597)** provides interpretable structure: hard items favor 2024, easy items favor 2023.
44	7. **Honest handling of limitations.** GSM1K correlation hedged as exploratory (N=33). Bifactor failure disclosed. RL robustness acknowledged as underpowered.
45	
46	#### Weaknesses
47	1. **[Major] Proposition 1 is narrower than presented.** The ΔFPR=0 guarantee holds when contamination is injected into a single subject s at a time. When contamination affects multiple subjects simultaneously (realistic scenario), the cross-subject matching variable for subject s IS affected by contamination in other subjects (since those items contribute to M_i^{(−s)}). The paper only tests single-subject injection. This limitation should be stated explicitly.
48	2. **[Major] The "31% temporal DIF" framing leaves the actionability gap unresolved.** The matched-ability analysis shows ~29% genuine DIF. But the paper simultaneously argues ρ=0.997 (rankings barely change) and z=8.5 (systematic cohort bias). The audit protocol is a half-page sketch with vague thresholds ("C%>20% triggers review"). The decision-theoretic grounding—what should a benchmark maintainer actually *do* when 31% of items are flagged—is underdeveloped.
49	3. **[Minor] Semi-synthetic injection (incorrect→correct flipping) is a simplification.** Real contamination could produce more subtle patterns (partial memorization, format-specific biases). ΔTPR=+0.508 should be contextualized as an upper bound for this injection mechanism. Acknowledged in Limitations but not sufficiently in the main text.
50	4. **[Minor] Cross-benchmark "replication" shares the model population.** All six benchmarks use overlapping models from METABENCH. This is replication across item sets with shared examinees, not fully independent replication. The claim should be hedged accordingly.
51	5. **[Minor] Bootstrap CI [29.4%, 38.1%] is asymmetric.** Upper tail extends 6.8pp above the point estimate vs 1.9pp below, suggesting right-skewed distribution and potential sensitivity to item subsets. Not discussed.
52	6. **[Minor] "FPR" is used in two senses.** In §4.5, FPR(clean)=0.318 is the rate at which DIF flags items Li et al. classify as "clean" (but the paper argues these labels are wrong). In the semi-synthetic framework, ΔFPR measures false positive inflation from injection. Using the same abbreviation for different quantities is confusing.
53	
54	#### Questions for Authors
55	1. How does Proposition 1 behave under simultaneous multi-subject contamination?
56	2. The ability-quintile C%=2.9% is 16× above random-split C%=0.18%. What explains this gap if ability differences alone produce it?
57	3. 6-month window C%=34.35% > full-year 31.3%. Why does a shorter window yield *more* DIF?
58	4. Why were only 5 of 57 subjects used for within-subject DIF? How were they selected?
59	
60	#### R1 Scores
61	- Soundness: 4/5
62	- Excitement: 3/5
63	- Reproducibility: 4/5
64	- Overall: 3.5 (Borderline Conference)
65	- Confidence: 4/5
66	
67	---
68	
69	### R2: Novelty & Related Work
70	
71	#### Strengths
72	1. **Scale and thoroughness of controls** are substantially more rigorous than typical "we applied method X and found result Y" papers. The six-confound elimination with convergence at ~29% is the methodological backbone.
73	2. **Ground truth meta-critique is genuinely insightful.** The distinction between global exposure and differential functioning identifies a systematic blind spot in the contamination detection literature's validation methodology. This insight extends beyond this paper.
74	3. **Simpson's paradox finding is non-trivial.** The OR reversal (1.55→0.73) is not something one would expect a priori. Genuinely changes interpretation of the 31% headline number.
75	4. **Cross-subject matching / Proposition 1** is practically valuable despite being conceptually straightforward. Clean ΔFPR=0.000 result.
76	5. **"Instability ≠ invalidity" framing is mature.** The paper resists overclaiming. The circularity ablation (DIF is the *least* disruptive non-random removal strategy) demonstrates intellectual honesty.
77	6. **Cross-benchmark replication** with interpretable task-type dichotomy (knowledge 27–31% > reasoning 18–19%) adds generalizability.
78	
79	#### Weaknesses
80	1. **[Major] Core methodological contribution is primarily application, not innovation.** MH-DIF is a 1988 method. ETS classification is standard. Cross-subject matching has psychometric precedent (Nandakumar 1993, Donoghue & Allen 1993). Proposition 1 is mathematically trivial—removing a variable from the conditioning set eliminates its influence by definition. Labeling this a formal "Proposition" overstates the intellectual contribution.
81	2. **[Major] The "so what" is not fully resolved.** If ρ=0.997, why should practitioners care? The audit protocol is a sketch, not a demonstrated improvement. The paper describes a phenomenon but does not show that acting on DIF findings improves benchmarks, evaluation, or models. The diagnostic lens framing is intellectually interesting but stops at description.
82	3. **[Major] Causal ambiguity limits impact.** DIF detects *that* items function differently, not *why*. Is instability driven by contamination? Training methodology shifts? Data mixture composition? Without causal attribution, 31% is descriptive phenomenology. The GSM1K anti-correlation (r=−0.478, N=33) is correctly marked exploratory but provides only weak evidence for one pathway.
83	4. **[Minor] Related Work has coverage gaps.** Missing engagement with: (a) benchmark saturation literature (Ott et al. 2022 in bib but not in text), (b) MMLU-Pro and other MMLU successors addressing item quality, (c) measurement invariance beyond DIF (multi-group CFA), (d) item parameter drift literature in educational measurement (Goldstein 1983, Wells et al. 2002), (e) IRT applications to NLP (Lalor et al. 2019, Rodriguez et al. 2021). Only 8 in-text references is thin for EMNLP.
84	5. **[Minor] Proposition 1 assumes cross-subject ability is independent of injected contamination.** In a multidimensional ability space (57 MMLU subjects), contamination in subject X could affect the matching variable computed from subjects Y,Z if latent factor correlations are strong.
85	6. **[Minor] Table 5 (cross-benchmark) lacks bootstrap CIs** for all benchmarks except MMLU. Unclear whether knowledge vs. reasoning C% differences are statistically significant.
86	7. **[Minor] Discussion/Conclusion is compressed.** §5 Limitations + §6 Conclusion occupy ~0.7 pages total. The audit protocol, relationship to concurrent work, and implications for benchmark design all deserve more development.
87	
88	#### Novelty Verdict
89	The novelty profile is uneven. The core DIF analysis is textbook application. The ground truth meta-critique (§4.5) is the most novel intellectual contribution—identifying that the contamination detection literature's validation methodology is categorically mismatched is an insight the community needs. The Simpson's paradox and difficulty×direction interaction add genuine analytical novelty. Cross-subject matching is practically useful with honest acknowledgment of psychometric precedent. The combination of (a) unprecedented scale, (b) meta-critique, (c) Simpson's paradox, and (d) reusable validation framework constitutes a sufficient contribution, though closer to Findings than main conference.
90	
91	#### Related Work Gaps
92	- IRT applications to NLP (Lalor et al. 2019, Rodriguez et al. 2021)
93	- Benchmark dynamics / saturation (Kiela et al. 2021 Dynabench, Ott et al. 2022)
94	- MMLU-Pro and MMLU successors (Wang et al. 2024)
95	- Item parameter drift in educational measurement (Goldstein 1983, Wells et al. 2002)
96	- Measurement invariance beyond DIF (Meredith 1993 / Vandenberg 2000 in bib but not discussed)
97	- Contamination detection beyond Min-K%/ConStat/DVD (Golchin & Surdeanu 2023, Shi et al. 2024)
98	
99	#### R2 Scores
100	- Soundness: 4/5
101	- Excitement: 3/5
102	- Reproducibility: 4/5
103	- Overall: 3.0 (Findings)
104	- Confidence: 4/5
105	
106	---
```

## plan_035_q3_inflation_sim 完成 ✅ (Round 5 第二优先级)

**Hypothesis**: LD (局部依赖) 对 MH-DIF Type I error 的膨胀程度

**方法**: Permutation null (1000 次) + Independence control，8 个 subjects（覆盖 Q3 从 4.6% 到 34.2%），cross-subject matching

**核心结果 — LD 膨胀几乎为零：**

| Subject | Q3>0.20 (%) | ETS-C Δ (pp) | p<0.05 Δ (pp) |
|---------|-------------|-------------|---------------|
| moral_scenarios | 34.2% | −0.00001 | −0.0019 |
| human_aging | 20.5% | +0.0004 | −0.0018 |
| world_religions | 18.1% | +0.0006 | −0.0040 |
| marketing | 17.5% | **+0.0010** | −0.0019 |
| professional_law | 10.5% | +0.0003 | −0.0005 |
| college_medicine | 4.6% | +0.0005 | −0.0008 |

**结论**：
- ETS-C LD 膨胀：mean +0.0004pp，max +0.001pp → **远低于 3pp 阈值**
- p<0.05 标准：**无膨胀**（均为负值，−0.0019pp mean）
- Q3 与膨胀无显著相关（ρ=−0.24, p=0.57）

**两个保护机制**：
1. Cross-subject matching 变量不含当前 subject items → LD 无法污染 matching
2. ETS-C |Δ|≥1.5 阈值本身极保守（actual FPR ~0.1%，远低于 nominal 5%）

**Paper 用法**: 直接回应 Reviewer 对 LD violation 的担忧。可声明 "permutation simulation confirms LD inflation < 0.001pp under cross-subject matching"。

---

| plan_035 Q3 inflation sim | ✅ DONE | LD inflation < 0.001pp, cross-subject matching 完全隔离 |
| plan_036 Growing Pains lookup | ✅ DONE | FPC=Fixed Parameter Calibration, 非污染检测, 适合 Related Work 引用 |

```json
  "text": "**提分策略讨论请求**\n\n当前论文 Overall 3.5 (Borderline Conference)，目标 4.0。DDL 5/25（3.5 天）。\n\nRound 5 审稿 + Dream #7/#8 一致指向 3.5。P17-P26 修复了所有 Must-Fix 和 Should-Fix，但分数可能仍在 3.5 区间。\n\n**请你独立评估**：\n\n1. **当前论文最薄弱的环节是什么？** 从 3.5→4.0，blocking factor 是什么？\n   - Novelty ceiling（MH-DIF 1988，应用不是创新）？\n   - 缺 ConStat/DVD head-to-head？\n   - Actionability（ρ=0.997 \"so what\"）？\n   - 其他？\n\n2. **3.5 天内最高 ROI 的提分手段是什么？** 选项包括：\n   - (a) 跑 ConStat on METABENCH（CPU，可能 6-12h，直接回应 reviewer #1 gap）\n   - (b) 更多 related work 覆盖（纯写作，2-3h）\n   - (c) 方法改进（不现实？或有 quick win？）\n   - (d) 论文写作质量 polish（narrative tightening）\n   - (e) 增加 display items（visual density）\n   - (f) 其他你想到的\n\n3. **6 个阴性实验有没有值得回头看的 failure case？** 哪些 negative 指向方法改进方向而非死胡同？\n\n4. **你认为 3.5→4.0 在 3.5 天内是 realistic 还是应该 lock in 3.5 准备提交？**\n\n请给出你的独立判断，不需要迎合我的乐观预期。",
```
